### 🤖 Antigravity AI

I have analyzed both the downloaded repository at `C:\Users\manoj2403\Downloads\BCA IGNOU app dev by bca.nextsem` and our current workspace `c:\jbrs-landing-page`. Here are my findings and reasoning on how we can merge the project cleanly with zero duplication and a premium, cohesive design.

### Key Findings & Analysis

1. **How the Downloaded Project Works**:
   * It is a client-side vanilla JavaScript/HTML Single Page Application (SPA).
   * It relies on `courses.json` and `unit_titles.json` to organize semester-by-semester textbooks (PDFs).
   * It features an **Interactive Learning Suite** modal (for BCS-011) that contains custom timelines, matching games, motherboard explorers, an SVG-based functional block diagram, a Von Neumann CPU simulator, a virtual logic gate breadboard, and a virtual DOS console.
   * It has a built-in PDF textbook reader with a collapsible sidebar for outlines, a Gemini-powered AI Study Buddy, and a digital drawing canvas for pen/highlight annotations saved to `localStorage`.

2. **Existing Workspace Base**:
   * Our current project is a Next.js 16 app powered by React 19 and Tailwind CSS v4, compiled as a static export (`output: 'export'`) deployed to Firebase.
   * The actual textbook PDFs and HTML files are located in `c:\jbrs-landing-page\BCA_NEW_Books` at the root, which means they are not compiled or served.
   * Our codebase already has clean, modular React components for several interactive guides (like `WindowsHistoryTimeline.tsx`, `CommandInterpreterSimulation.tsx`, `TabbedOS.tsx`).

---

### Propose Merge Strategy (Zero Duplicity & Matching Design)

* **Textbook Access**: Move the `BCA_NEW_Books` directory from the root level to `public/BCA_NEW_Books`. In Next.js static exports, this ensures all PDFs and HTMLs are served at the root of staging and production at `/BCA_NEW_Books/` path, matching the data mapping.
* **Metadata Schema**: Combine the semesters/subjects structures from `courses_data.js` and `unit_titles.json` into a single TypeScript file `src/data/bcaCoursesData.ts`. We will clean up the paths, converting absolute paths containing hardcoded local drives (e.g. `D:\`) into relative `/BCA_NEW_Books/` URLs.
* **Sleek Tailwind Styling**: Discard the downloaded `styles.css` entirely. We will construct all routers and simulation modules natively using Tailwind CSS, fitting them perfectly within the dark-themed aesthetic, using glassmorphic card grids, hover transitions, and glowing highlights (e.g. green `#39FF14` or purple `#8b5cf6`).
* **Interactive Suite Ports**: Port the remaining simulations (generations matching game, motherboard board explorer, CPU simulator, logic gate breadboard, virtual console) into reusable React components in `src/components/bca-interactive/`. When users select BCS-011 Block 1 Unit 1/2, they will trigger a custom modal presenting these interactive sandbox widgets.
* **Unified Progress & AI Buddy**: Integrate the PDF viewing progress and AI Study Buddy directly with our current Firebase auth and Firestore `users_progress` collection, keeping a single point-tally and profile for each student.

We split them into Server wrappers (`page.tsx`) with `generateStaticParams()` and Client controllers (`SemesterClient.tsx` and `SubjectClient.tsx`) to support static HTML compilations.
4. **Interactive Lab Workspace**: Built a master container modal ([BcaInteractiveModal.tsx](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/components/bca-interactive/BcaInteractiveModal.tsx)) hosting 7 custom sandbox stations:

5. **Unified Viewer Panel**: Split the viewer into a static `Suspense` page wrapper ([page.tsx](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/bca/viewer/page.tsx)) and client workspace ([ViewerClient.tsx](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/bca/viewer/ViewerClient.tsx)), enabling:

   * Floating Digital Draw Pen canvas overlay (draws, highlights, erases, and persists annotations to `localStorage` keyed by PDF path).
   * Completed unit toggles synced directly with the user's unified `ProgressContext` profile.
6. **Compilation Success**: Next.js compiled all 139 static pages, sitemaps, and sitemap.xml verify checks cleanly.

### What Was Corrected:
1. **Refactored to Unified SVG**: We converted the hybrid HTML overlays (Input, CPU, RAM, and Output) into standard SVG shapes (`<rect>`, `<g>`, `<text>`) inside a single master responsive vector element (`viewBox="0 0 480 320"`).
2. **Fixed Clipping & Overlap**: Because everything is drawn in vector space, the browser will dynamically scale down the entire diagram to fit inside whatever column width is allocated by the parent containers.
3. **Restored Memory Lines & Dynamic Text**: We mapped and rendered memory array values (split by `<br/>`) and output unit statuses as SVG `<text>` elements, maintaining the exact styling and interactive click actions.

The fixed layout is now fully live on your production domain [https://nextsem.online/bca](https://nextsem.online/bca)!

- **Page 3**: 1970 APB definition (providing quantitative financial information). Summary of definitions (Economic events, Identification/Measurement/Recording/Communication, Organisation, Interested Users). Box 1: History and Development of Accounting (Babylonia/Egypt clay tablets 4000 BC, Wazirs, Greece financial allocations, Romans memorandum/daybook/ledger, China 2000 BC, India Kautilya's Arthashastra, Luca Pacioli 1494 Venice double-entry book-keeping).
- **Page 4**: History continued (Debit from 'debito/debita/debeo' - owed to proprietor, Credit from 'credito/credo' - trust/belief/owed by proprietor). 1.1.1 Economic Events: external events (with outsiders, e.g. sale, purchase, rent) vs internal events (within enterprise, e.g. raw material supply from stores to manufacturing, wages).
- **Page 5**: 1.1.2 Identification, Measurement, Recording and Communication.
  - Identification: determining what to record (observing financial character). Examples: value of HR or manager changes NOT recorded; cash/credit sales/purchases and salaries ARE recorded.
  - Measurement: quantification in monetary units (rupees/paise). Unquantified items (new MD, contracts, changes in personnel) NOT recorded.
  - Recording: chronological, summarized books.
  - Communication: reports (daily, weekly, monthly, quarterly) sent to internal & external users.
- **Page 6**: 1.1.3 Organisation: profit or not-for-profit enterprise (sole proprietorship, partnership, company, cooperative, local authority, etc.). 1.1.4 Interested Users: Internal (CEO, CFO, managers, supervisors) vs External (investors, creditors/banks, debenture holders, tax authorities, SEBI, registrar, unions, customers, etc.). Box 2: Why do users want information (Owners/shareholders for return & health; Directors/managers for internal/external comparison, strengths/weaknesses).
- **Page 7**: Box 2 continued (Creditors/lenders for liquidity; prospective investors for whether to invest; government for tax liabilities and compliance with Companies Act 2013 and SEBI). 1.2 Accounting as a Source of Information: starting with transaction identification, ending with financial statements. Disseminating to users to make economic decisions, cash flow timing, resource utilization, social impact.
- **Page 8**: Test Your Understanding - I (fill in the blanks). Role of accountant. Sub-disciplines: Financial accounting, Cost accounting, Management accounting. Financial accounting assists in recording, preparing, and presenting reports for organizational success and soundness (stewardship function). Cost accounting: expenditure analysis, cost ascertainment, price fixation, and cost control.
- **Page 9**: Management accounting: planning, budgeting, decision-making (sales forecast, cash flows, manpower, environmental data like flora, fauna, human health). New areas: HR accounting, social accounting, responsibility accounting. 1.2.1 Qualitative Characteristics of Accounting Information: Reliability, Relevance, Understandability, Comparability. Reliability: free from error and bias, verifiable, neutral, and faithful.
- **Page 10**: Box 3: Branches of Accounting (Financial, Cost, Management). Relevance: available in time, predictive/feedback value, influences decisions. Understandability: interpreting in the same sense as prepared.
- **Page 11**: Qualitative Characteristics chart (Figure 1.3). Comparability: comparing across time periods or other entities.
- **Page 12**: Test Your Understanding - II. 1.3 Objectives of Accounting: Maintenance of records of business transactions, Calculation of profit and loss.
- **Page 13**: Depiction of financial position (Assets and Liabilities/Balance Sheet). Providing accounting information to users (internal/external details: investors, unions, lenders, suppliers, customers).
- **Page 14**: Stakeholders continued (government, social responsibility groups, competitors). Test Your Understanding - III. 1.4 Role of Accounting: language of business, historical record, info system, limitations (past transactions only, quantitative/financial only, no qualitative/non-financial info).
- **Page 15**: Test Your Understanding - IV. Box 4: Different Roles of Accounting (Language, Historical record, Current economic reality, Information system, Commodity).
- **Page 16**: 1.5 Basic Terms. 1.5.1 Entity (Super Bazaar, Hire Jewellers). 1.5.2 Transaction (cash/credit). 1.5.3 Assets (current & non-current).
- **Page 17**: Figure 1.4: Classification of Assets (Fixed assets: Tangible/Intangible/Capital WIP; Deferred Tax Assets; Long Term Loans; Other Non-current; Current Assets: Current Investment, Inventories, Trade Receivables, Cash & cash equivalents, Short term loans, Other current).

Let's read Chapter 3 Current Electricity contents from the OCR text:
- **3.1 Introduction / 3.2 Electric Current**: Current is flow of charge. $q = q_+ - q_-$. Quotient $I = q/t$. More generally, instantaneous current is $I(t) = \lim_{\Delta t \to 0} \frac{\Delta Q}{\Delta t}$. SI unit: Ampere (A). Average lightning: tens of thousands of Amperes; nerves: microamperes.
- **3.3 Electric Currents in Conductors**: In metals, valence electrons are free to move. Under thermal motion (no electric field), average velocity of electrons is zero because collision directions are random. Under electric field, electrons accelerate with $\vec{a} = \frac{-e\vec{E}}{m}$.
- **3.4 Ohm's Law**: $V \propto I \implies V = RI$. Resistance $R$ depends on material and dimensions. $R \propto l$ and $R \propto 1/A \implies R = \rho \frac{l}{A}$ where $\rho$ is resistivity. Current density $j = I/A$. Potential difference $V = El$. Cast Ohm's law in vector form: $\vec{E} = \rho \vec{j}$ or $\vec{j} = \sigma \vec{E}$, where conductivity $\sigma = 1/\rho$.
- **3.5 Drift of Electrons and Origin of Resistivity**: Immediate velocity after collision is random. Time between successive collisions on average is relaxation time $\tau$. Drift velocity $\vec{v}_d = \frac{-e\vec{E}}{m}\tau$. Current $I = n e A v_d$. Current density $j = n e v_d = \frac{n e^2 \tau}{m}E \implies \sigma = \frac{n e^2 \tau}{m}$ and resistivity $\rho = \frac{m}{n e^2 \tau}$.
- **Mobility**: $\mu = \frac{|\vec{v}_d|}{E} = \frac{e\tau}{m}$. SI unit: $m^2 V^{-1} s^{-1}$.
- **3.6 Limitations of Ohm's Law**:
  - $V$ ceases to be proportional to $I$ at high currents (thermal heating).
  - Relation depends on sign of $V$ (diode characteristics).
  - Relation is not unique (e.g., GaAs has a negative resistance region).
- **3.8 Temperature Dependence of Resistivity**: $\rho_T = \rho_0 [1 + \alpha(T-T_0)]$. $\alpha$ is temperature coefficient of resistivity. Dimensional unit: $(^\circ C)^{-1}$ or $K^{-1}$.
  - Metals: $\alpha$ is positive (resistivity increases with temperature because $\tau$ decreases).
  - Semiconductors/Insulators: resistivity decreases with temperature (because charge carrier density $n$ increases exponentially, compensating for any decrease in $\tau$).
  - Alloys (Nichrome, Manganin, Constantan): very weak temperature dependence (used in wire-bound standard resistors).
- **3.9 Electrical Energy, Power**: Potential energy change $\Delta U_{pot} = -I V \Delta t$. Power dissipated $P = I V = I^2 R = V^2/R$. High voltage power transmission minimizes power loss in transmission lines ($P_c = \frac{P^2 R_c}{V^2}$, power loss is inversely proportional to $V^2$).
- **3.10 Cells, EMF, Internal Resistance**: Electrolytic cell maintains steady current. Emf $\varepsilon = V_+ + V_- > 0$ (open circuit potential difference). In closed circuit with current $I$, terminal potential difference $V = \varepsilon - Ir$. Current in circuit $I = \frac{\varepsilon}{R + r}$. Maximum current $I_{max} = \varepsilon/r$ when external resistance $R=0$.
- **3.11 Cells in Series and Parallel**:
  - Series: $\varepsilon_{eq} = \varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2$, $r_{eq} = r_1 + r_2$. If connected in opposition (negatives together), $\varepsilon_{eq} = \varepsilon_1 - \varepsilon_2$ (for $\varepsilon_1 > \varepsilon_2$).
  - Parallel: $\frac{1}{r_{eq}} = \frac{1}{r_1} + \frac{1}{r_2}$, $\frac{\varepsilon_{eq}}{r_{eq}} = \frac{\varepsilon_1}{r_1} + \frac{\varepsilon_2}{r_2}$.
- **3.12 Kirchhoff's Rules**:
  - Junction Rule: conservation of charge. Sum of currents entering a junction equals sum of currents leaving it.
  - Loop Rule: conservation of energy. Sum of potential changes around any closed loop is zero.
- **3.13 Wheatstone Bridge**: Four resistors in a loop. Balanced when current through galvanometer $I_g = 0$. Condition: $\frac{R_2}{R_1} = \frac{R_4}{R_3} \implies R_4 = R_3 \frac{R_2}{R_1}$. Used in meter bridge.

### 1. Isolated NCERT Courses & Database
* **Database Isolation:** Created a dedicated database file [ncertCoursesData.ts](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/data/ncertCoursesData.ts) containing the metadata structure for the NCERT prep classes (Class 11 Accountancy and Class 12 Physics).
* **Cleaned BCA Directory:** Reverted [bcaCoursesData.ts](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/data/bcaCoursesData.ts) to remove high school prep subjects, keeping it dedicated to BCA college course curriculum.

### 2. Main Dashboard & Hub Routing
* **Homepage Integration:** Created the **NCERT Syllabus Hub** module card on the homepage in [HomeClient.tsx](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/HomeClient.tsx), featuring a custom SVG illustration, progress calculators, and route triggers that take the user to `/ncert`.
* **Separate Route Hub:** Created the server page wrapper [/ncert/page.tsx](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/ncert/page.tsx) and client dashboard [NcertClient.tsx](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/ncert/NcertClient.tsx) displaying the school subjects and blocks, which routes to the active notes.
* **Unified Redirects:** Updated the redirection rules in [ViewerClient.tsx](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/bca/viewer/ViewerClient.tsx) to map these NCERT units to `/ncert/notes/` routes.

### 3. Interactive Revision Notes Guides
Moved and styled the three notes chapters under the new `/ncert/notes/` directory:
* **[accounting-chapter-1](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/ncert/notes/accounting-chapter-1/page.tsx):** Introduction to Accounting revision guide + self-tests.
* **[accounting-chapter-2](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/ncert/notes/accounting-chapter-2/page.tsx):** Theory Base of Accounting revision guide + GST (CGST/SGST/IGST) interactive breakdown charts.
* **[physics-chapter-3](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/ncert/notes/physics-chapter-3/page.tsx):** Physics Current Electricity revision guide + Ohm's Law vector form, drift velocity, and Kirchhoff's loop/junction rule self-tests.

* **Progress Checkboxes:** Integrated progress checkboxes at the bottom of each revision notes page, enabling students to check off chapters and sync completion states to local storage.

### 4. Build, Deploy & Archive
* **Verified Compilation:** Ran `npm run build` which verified that all TypeScript typings and Next.js static layouts compile cleanly.
* **Production Deployment:** Deployed the changes to production via `npm run deploy:prod` (hosting URL: `https://bca-nextsem.web.app`).
* **Chat Logging:** Ran `npm run save-chat` to archive the session log under the `chat-logs/` folder.

Let's make sure these three checks are in `handleLaunchChapter`:
```typescript
  const handleLaunchChapter = (path: string) => {
    if (path === "cmd") {
      router.push("/cmd");
    } else if (path === "bca") {
      router.push("/bca");
    } else if (path === "ncert") {
      router.push("/ncert");
    } else if (path === "playground") {
      router.push("/playground");
    } else if (path === "simulator") {
      router.push("/simulator");
    } else if (path === "semmy-lab") {
      router.push("/bca/semmy-ai");
    } else {
      router.push(`/book/${path}`);
    }
  };
```
This is beautifully simple!

            if (mod.id === 'bca') {

                    if (completedChapters[`ncert_unit:${noteKey}`]) {
                      read++;
                    }
                  });
                });
              });
              modTotalCount = total;
              modCompletedCount = read;

              const { isSimulatorComplete } = require('@/data/desktopSimulatorSteps');
              modTotalCount = 1;
              modCompletedCount = isSimulatorComplete(completedChapters) ? 1 : 0;
            } else if (mod.id === 'playground') {
              modTotalCount = 1;
              modCompletedCount = completedChapters['playground_visited'] ? 1 : 0;
            } else if (mod.id === 'semmy-lab') {
              modTotalCount = 1;
              modCompletedCount = completedChapters['semmy_trained'] ? 1 : 0;
            }

Let's design `renderCard` exactly:
```typescript
  const renderCard = (mod: ModuleItem) => {
    const modPhase = coursePhases.find(p => p.id === mod.id.toUpperCase());
    const modTopics = modPhase?.topics || [];
    
    let modCompletedCount = modTopics.filter(t => completedChapters[t.path]).length;
    let modTotalCount = modTopics.length;
    
    if (mod.id === 'bca') {
      const { bcaSemesters } = require('@/data/bcaCoursesData');
      let total = 0;
      let read = 0;
      bcaSemesters.forEach((sem: any) => {
        sem.subjects.forEach((sub: any) => {
          sub.blocks.forEach((block: any) => {
            block.units.forEach((unit: any) => {
              total++;
              if (completedChapters[`bca_unit:${block.path}/${unit.fileName}`]) {
                read++;
              }
            });
          });
        });
      });
      modTotalCount = total;
      modCompletedCount = read;
    } else if (mod.id === 'ncert') {
      const { ncertSubjects } = require('@/data/ncertCoursesData');
      let total = 0;
      let read = 0;
      ncertSubjects.forEach((sub: any) => {
        sub.blocks.forEach((block: any) => {
          block.units.forEach((unit: any) => {
            total++;
            let noteKey = 'accounting-chapter-1';
            if (unit.fileName.toLowerCase().includes('theory base')) noteKey = 'accounting-chapter-2';
            if (unit.fileName.toLowerCase().includes('current electricity')) noteKey = 'physics-chapter-3';
            
            if (completedChapters[`ncert_unit:${noteKey}`]) {
              read++;
            }
          });
        });
      });
      modTotalCount = total;
      modCompletedCount = read;
    } else if (mod.id === 'simulator') {
      const { isSimulatorComplete } = require('@/data/desktopSimulatorSteps');
      modTotalCount = 1;
      modCompletedCount = isSimulatorComplete(completedChapters) ? 1 : 0;
    } else if (mod.id === 'playground') {
      modTotalCount = 1;
      modCompletedCount = completedChapters['playground_visited'] ? 1 : 0;
    } else if (mod.id === 'semmy-lab') {
      modTotalCount = 1;
      modCompletedCount = completedChapters['semmy_trained'] ? 1 : 0;
    }
    
    const modPercent = modTotalCount > 0 ? Math.round((modCompletedCount / modTotalCount) * 100) : 0;
    const modNextTopic = modTopics.find(t => !completedChapters[t.path]) || modTopics[0] || { label: mod.title, path: mod.firstChapterId };
    const isCompleted = modPercent === 100;
    const isSandbox = mod.category === 'sandbox';

    return (
      <div
        key={mod.id}
        className="bg-[#111116]/80 hover:bg-[#14141d] border border-white/5 hover:border-emerald-500/20 rounded-[28px] p-6 flex flex-col justify-between transition-all duration-300 hover:-translate-y-1 shadow-lg group relative overflow-hidden text-left"
      >
        {/* Accent glow on hover */}
        <div
          className="absolute top-0 right-0 w-32 h-32 rounded-full blur-[40px] opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-10 pointer-events-none transition-all duration-500"
          style={{ backgroundColor: mod.color }}
        />

        {/* SVG Illustration & Header */}
        <div>
          <div className="w-full h-32 flex items-center justify-center relative mb-4 shrink-0 transition-transform duration-300 group-hover:scale-105">
            {mod.renderIllustration()}
          </div>

          <div className="text-left mt-2">
            <span className="inline-block text-[8px] font-black uppercase tracking-widest px-2 py-0.5 rounded bg-white/5 text-slate-400 mb-1">
              {isSandbox ? 'Utility Sandbox' : (mod.id === 'cli' ? 'Prerequisite' : `Phase ${mod.id}`)}
            </span>
            <h3 className="text-base font-black text-white leading-tight">{mod.title}</h3>
            <p className="text-[10px] text-slate-500 font-bold leading-normal mt-1">{mod.subtitle}</p>
          </div>
        </div>

        {/* Bottom section: Progress bar + CTA */}
        <div className="mt-6 space-y-4 pt-4 border-t border-white/5">
          {/* Mini Progress */}
          <div className="flex items-center justify-between text-[9px] font-black text-slate-400">
            <span>{isSandbox ? (isCompleted ? 'Completed' : 'Not Started') : `${modCompletedCount} / ${modTotalCount} Lessons`}</span>
            <span className="text-[#39FF14]">{modPercent}%</span>
          </div>
          <div className="w-full h-1 bg-slate-800 rounded-full overflow-hidden">
            <div
              className="h-full rounded-full transition-all duration-500"
              style={{
                width: `${modPercent}%`,
                backgroundColor: mod.color
              }}
            />
          </div>

          {/* Action Button */}
          <button
            onClick={() => handleLaunchChapter(modNextTopic.path)}
            className="w-full py-3 rounded-2xl font-black text-[10px] uppercase tracking-wider transition-all duration-200 cursor-pointer flex items-center justify-center gap-1.5 shadow-md"
            style={{
              backgroundColor: isCompleted ? 'rgba(57,255,20,0.1)' : '#ffffff',
              color: isCompleted ? '#39FF14' : '#07070c',
              border: isCompleted ? '1px solid rgba(57,255,20,0.2)' : 'none'
            }}
          >
            {isCompleted ? (
              <>
                <span>Review Phase</span>
              </>
            ) : (
              <>
                <span>{isSandbox ? 'Launch Sandbox' : 'Continue Lesson'}</span>
                <span className="font-sans">&rarr;</span>
              </>
            )}
          </button>
        </div>
      </div>
    );
  };
```

### 🤖 Antigravity AI

The deployment is fully complete and type errors have been fixed! Let's explain this to the user.I found and resolved the issue! 

### What went wrong:
The crash was caused by a client-side TypeScript hydration error on the staging build. We were using dynamic `require` statements (`require('@/data/bcaCoursesData')` etc.) inside the render mapping loop to fetch course metadata on the client, which throws an exception during Next.js production/staging rendering and causes Chrome Custom Tabs / PWA WebView to fail loading with the *"This page couldn't load"* error.

Additionally, `isSimulatorComplete` expects a list of completed steps (`string[]`) as its parameter, whereas the code was passing a `Record<string, boolean>`, leading to a compilation mismatch.

---

### How I fixed it:
1. **Static ESM Imports:** Replaced the dynamic `require` calls inside the mapping loop with clean, static ES Module imports at the top of [HomeClient.tsx](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/HomeClient.tsx).
2. **Proper LocalStorage Parsing:** Scoped and fetched the user-specific terminal step array (`nextsem_terminal_step_${email}`) from `localStorage` on the homepage, parsed it safely, and sent it as a clean `string[]` to `isSimulatorComplete`.
3. **Clean Rebuild & Successful Staging Deploy:** Compiled the code successfully and deployed the updated build to:
   👉 **[https://nextsem-staging.web.app](https://nextsem-staging.web.app)**

The staging site is now fully functional, loads correctly, and renders the new **Structured Core Curriculums** and **Interactive Sandboxes & AI Tools** shelves without any hydration issues!

### 👤 User

i have a question is sub-domain allowed under my nextsem.online hostinger hosting ?

---

### 🤖 Antigravity AI

Yes, **subdomains are absolutely allowed** under Hostinger hosting for your domain `nextsem.online`! 

The number of subdomains you can create depends on your Hostinger plan:
* **Single Shared Hosting:** Allows up to **10 subdomains**.
* **Premium Shared Hosting:** Allows up to **100 subdomains**.
* **Business Shared Hosting:** Allows up to **100 subdomains**.
* **Cloud Hosting:** Allows up to **100 subdomains** per domain.

---

### How to use/set them up (Two Scenarios):

#### Scenario A: You want to host files on Hostinger itself (e.g. `sandbox.nextsem.online`)
If you want to upload separate files directly to Hostinger's servers under a subdomain:
1. Log in to your **Hostinger hPanel**.
2. Go to **Websites** ➔ click **Manage** next to `nextsem.online`.
3. In the left sidebar, search for **Subdomains** (under the *Domains* section).
4. Enter the subdomain name (e.g. `sandbox`).
5. **Tip:** Check the box *"Create a custom folder for subdomain"* so it creates a clean directory in your File Manager (like `public_html/sandbox`) separate from your main website files.
6. Click **Create**. You can now upload your subdomain's static files into that folder!

#### Scenario B: You want to point the subdomain to another service (e.g. Firebase, Vercel, or Netlify)
If you want to host your subdomain elsewhere, **you do not need to create it in the Subdomains section**. You just need to add a DNS record:
1. In your hPanel, go to **DNS / Nameservers** (or search for *DNS Zone Editor*).
2. Add a new record:
   * **Type:** `CNAME` (or `A` depending on the hosting provider).
   * **Name:** Enter the prefix (e.g. `sandbox` if you want `sandbox.nextsem.online`).
   * **Target (Points to):** Paste the URL provided by Firebase/Vercel (e.g. `cname.vercel-dns.com` or your Firebase Hosting project custom domain configuration CNAME).
   * **TTL:** Keep it default (usually 14400 or 3600).
3. Click **Add Record**. Once DNS propagates (takes 10 mins to a few hours), it will load your separate host successfully!

```typescript
export async function askGemini(prompt: string, apiKey: string): Promise<string> {
  const url = `https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models/gemini-1.5-flash:generateContent?key=${apiKey}`;
  const response = await fetch(url, {
    method: "POST",
    headers: {
      "Content-Type": "application/json"
    },
    body: JSON.stringify({
      contents: [{
        parts: [{ text: prompt }]
      }],
      systemInstruction: "You are 'Semmy', an encouraging AI tutor mascot for Nextsem Academy. You help students learn web development (HTML, CSS, JS, React, Node.js) and computer fundamentals (Von Neumann, logic gates, CPU registers) and school preps (Class 11 Accounting, Class 12 Current Electricity). Keep your answers concise, clear, and structured with bullet points. Suggest commands or concepts where applicable."
    })
  });
  
  if (!response.ok) {
    throw new Error("Gemini API call failed");
  }
  
  const data = await response.json();
  return data.candidates?.[0]?.content?.parts?.[0]?.text || "Sorry, I couldn't process that.";
}
```

Wait! If `concept` is not found, we can query the Gemini API!
Let's see: how do we fetch the Gemini API key?
We can fetch:
- `localStorage.getItem('nextsem_gemini_api_key')`
- `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_GEMINI_API_KEY`

If the key is available, we can trigger the async call to Gemini!
Since the `setTimeout` callback is synchronous, we can rewrite this function to be async, or call a separate helper `handleGeminiResponse(query)`.
Let's design the response flow:
```typescript
    setTimeout(async () => {
      const concept = querySemmy(query, kb);

      if (concept) {
        setIsTyping(false);
        const reply: ChatMessage = {
          id: (Date.now() + 1).toString(),
          sender: 'semmy',
          text: `**${concept.title}**\n\n${concept.summary}`,
          hasCode: !!concept.code,
          code: concept.code,
        };
        setMessages(prev => [...prev, reply]);
      } else {
        // Try calling Gemini API dynamically
        const localKey = typeof window !== 'undefined' ? localStorage.getItem('nextsem_gemini_api_key') : null;
        const apiKey = localKey || process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_GEMINI_API_KEY;

        if (apiKey) {
          try {
            const replyText = await askGemini(query, apiKey);
            setIsTyping(false);
            const reply: ChatMessage = {
              id: (Date.now() + 1).toString(),
              sender: 'semmy',
              text: replyText,
            };
            setMessages(prev => [...prev, reply]);
            return;
          } catch (err) {
            console.error("Gemini query failed, falling back to database", err);
          }
        }

        // Fallback if no API key or if API key failed
        setIsTyping(false);
        const notFoundMsg: ChatMessage = {
          id: (Date.now() + 1).toString(),
          sender: 'semmy',
          text: `I don't know about "${query}" yet! But you can teach me 🎓`,
          notFound: true,
          keyword: query
        };
        setMessages(prev => [...prev, notFoundMsg]);
        setHasNewMessage(true);
      }
    }, 900);
```
This is absolutely marvelous! If the key exists, it calls the Gemini API in real-time. If it doesn't exist, it falls back to the database-driven message where the student can "Teach Semmy" manually, which is extremely elegant and preserves both features concurrently!

```typescript
export async function askGemini(prompt: string, apiKey: string): Promise<string> {
  const url = `https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models/gemini-1.5-flash:generateContent?key=${apiKey}`;
  const response = await fetch(url, {
    method: "POST",
    headers: {
      "Content-Type": "application/json"
    },
    body: JSON.stringify({
      contents: [{
        parts: [{ text: prompt }]
      }],
      systemInstruction: "You are 'Semmy', an encouraging AI tutor mascot for Nextsem Academy. You help students learn web development (HTML, CSS, JS, React, Node.js), computer fundamentals (Von Neumann, CPU registers, digital logic gates), and school curriculum (Class 11 Accountancy, Class 12 Current Electricity). Keep your answers concise, structured, and easy to read. Suggest code, command prompt outputs, or concepts where applicable."
    })
  });
  
  if (!response.ok) {
    throw new Error("Gemini API call failed");
  }
  
  const data = await response.json();
  return data.candidates?.[0]?.content?.parts?.[0]?.text || "Sorry, I couldn't process that.";
}
```

---

### 1. `ncert.nextsem.online` Subdomain Support
* **Root Redirection:** Configured [HomeClient.tsx](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/HomeClient.tsx) initialization block to detect if the hostname starts with `ncert.`. If a visitor hits the homepage on `ncert.nextsem.online`, they are instantly and seamlessly redirected to the `/ncert` route.
* **Context-Aware Back Navigation:** Updated the header back link in [NcertClient.tsx](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/ncert/NcertClient.tsx) to be dynamic. 
  * If accessed via `ncert.nextsem.online`, the back button is labeled **"Back to Nextsem"** and links back to the main domain `https://nextsem.online`.
  * If accessed on the main site `/ncert`, it behaves normally as **"Back to Hub"** pointing to `/`.
  * Note: Subdomain isolation is complete since relative sub-notes paths `/ncert/notes/...` work natively.

---

### 2. Live Google Gemini API Integration for Chat Fallback
* **Created Gemini Helper:** Built a robust API helper in [gemini.ts](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/lib/gemini.ts) to execute client-side API requests to Google Gemini `1.5-flash` with a tailored educational tutor system instruction prompt.
* **Smart Fallback Handlers:** Updated both the floating chat widget ([SemmyAiWidget.tsx](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/components/SemmyAiWidget.tsx)) and the training lab console ([SemmyAiClient.tsx](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/bca/semmy-ai/SemmyAiClient.tsx)) to:
  1. Check the local compiled database first.
  2. If the concept is not found, check if a Gemini API Key exists.
  3. If yes, query Gemini in real-time to return a dynamic structured answer, eliminating the *"outside my knowledge"* fallback error entirely!
  4. If no API key is supplied, fall back safely to the manual train/teach suggestion.
* **Interactive API Key Manager:** Added a card under the **"Train"** tab in the Training Lab (`/bca/semmy-ai`) to allow inputting and clearing your Gemini API Key safely in `localStorage` without exposing it in the codebase.
* **Environment Variable Support:** Also enabled reading from `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_GEMINI_API_KEY` if you want to bundle it at build time.

       - Each thread item has a delete button (Trash icon) to remove it from Firestore.

     - **Chat Messages Scroller (`flex-1 overflow-y-auto px-4 md:px-12 py-8 space-y-6 custom-scrollbar`):**

          messages: updatedMessages,
          updatedAt: new Date().toISOString()
        }, { merge: true });
      }

- If the user deletes a thread:
  - Call `deleteDoc(doc(db, "users_chats", threadId))`.
  - Remove it from the `threads` list in state.
  - If `activeThreadId === threadId`, reset `activeThreadId` to null (start new chat).

Wait! Let's check: is there a Markdown parser or can we write a robust, clean regex-based renderer?
Writing a clean, custom inline renderer for markdown in React is very simple and robust for these specific formatting rules:
- Split content by code blocks ` ``` ` to identify code blocks vs text blocks.
- For text blocks:
  - Replace `\n` with `<br />` or map lines.
  - Render bullet lists (lines starting with `- ` or `* ` or numbers) as `ul`/`ol` list items.
  - Render bold text `**text**` as `<strong>text</strong>`.
  - Render headers `### text` or `## text` as `h3`/`h4` elements.
  - Since this is a React component, we can write a simple component `MarkdownText` that parses the string and returns JSX elements. This is extremely clean, safe, and avoids raw HTML injection vulnerabilities (`dangerouslySetInnerHTML`). Let's design a custom JSX-based markdown renderer! It's so much more elegant.

Wait! We should review the custom markdown formatter. How do we parse codes in Gemini replies?
Usually, Gemini wraps code blocks in ` ```typescript ` or ` ```javascript ` or ` ```html ` and ends them with ` ``` `.
We can parse them:
If the text contains ` ``` `, we can split by ` ``` `.
Every alternate block (odd index) is a code block. We can extract the language (the first word of the block) and render the rest as a code block.
For example:
```typescript
function parseGeminiMessage(text: string) {
  const parts = text.split("```");
  return parts.map((part, index) => {
    const isCode = index % 2 === 1;
    if (isCode) {
      const lines = part.split("\n");
      const language = lines[0].trim();
      const codeContent = lines.slice(1).join("\n").trim();
      return { isCode: true, language, content: codeContent };
    } else {
      return { isCode: false, content: part };
    }
  });
}
```
Oh! This is exceptionally robust and clean! It gives us an array of sections (text or code), which we can map to JSX elements.
For code sections: we render them in our custom code editor window with language tag, background, and copy button.
For text sections: we can render inline bolds `**`, headers `### `, and bullet items `- `.
Let's write a helper component `FormattedMessage` that renders this structure perfectly!
```typescript
const FormattedMessage = ({ text }: { text: string }) => {
  const parsed = parseGeminiMessage(text);
  
  return (
    <div className="space-y-4">
      {parsed.map((section, idx) => {
        if (section.isCode) {
          return (
            <CodeBlock 
              key={idx} 
              code={section.content} 
              language={section.language || "code"} 
            />
          );
        } else {
          return (
            <div key={idx} className="space-y-2 leading-relaxed text-sm text-slate-200">
              {section.content.split("\n").map((line, lIdx) => {
                const trimmed = line.trim();
                if (trimmed.startsWith("### ")) {
                  return (
                    <h3 key={lIdx} className="text-sm font-black text-white uppercase tracking-wider mt-4 mb-2 flex items-center gap-1.5">
                      <Sparkles size={12} className="text-indigo-400" />
                      {parseInlineBold(trimmed.substring(4))}
                    </h3>
                  );
                }
                if (trimmed.startsWith("## ")) {
                  return (
                    <h2 key={lIdx} className="text-base font-black text-white mt-4 mb-2 flex items-center gap-1.5">
                      {parseInlineBold(trimmed.substring(3))}
                    </h2>
                  );
                }
                if (trimmed.startsWith("- ") || trimmed.startsWith("* ")) {
                  return (
                    <div key={lIdx} className="flex items-start gap-2 ml-4 my-1">
                      <span className="text-[#39FF14] text-[10px] mt-1.5">•</span>
                      <span className="flex-1 text-slate-350">{parseInlineBold(trimmed.substring(2))}</span>
                    </div>
                  );
                }
                if (trimmed.match(/^\d+\.\s/)) {
                  const numberMatch = trimmed.match(/^(\d+)\.\s(.*)/);
                  const num = numberMatch ? numberMatch[1] : "";
                  const content = numberMatch ? numberMatch[2] : trimmed;
                  return (
                    <div key={lIdx} className="flex items-start gap-2 ml-4 my-1">
                      <span className="text-indigo-400 font-mono text-xs font-black">{num}.</span>
                      <span className="flex-1 text-slate-350">{parseInlineBold(content)}</span>
                    </div>
                  );
                }
                if (!trimmed) return <div key={lIdx} className="h-2" />;
                return <p key={lIdx} className="text-slate-305">{parseInlineBold(trimmed)}</p>;
              })}
            </div>
          );
        }
      })}
    </div>
  );
};
```
Wait! What about the inline bold helper `parseInlineBold`?
We can split the string by `**`:
```typescript
const parseInlineBold = (str: string) => {
  const parts = str.split("**");
  return parts.map((part, index) => {
    const isBold = index % 2 === 1;
    if (isBold) {
      return <strong key={index} className="font-extrabold text-white">{part}</strong>;
    }
    return part;
  });
};
```
Oh my goodness, this is so elegant! It does not require any third-party library, compile-time overhead, or unsafe raw HTML rendering. It is 100% pure React, extremely secure, and works beautifully with Gemini outputs!

Let's design the custom CodeBlock component with the "Copy Code" button:
```typescript
const CodeBlock = ({ code, language }: { code: string; language: string }) => {
  const [copied, setCopied] = useState(false);
  
  const handleCopy = async () => {
    try {
      await navigator.clipboard.writeText(code);
      setCopied(true);
      setTimeout(() => setCopied(false), 2000);
    } catch (err) {}
  };
  
  return (
    <div className="border border-white/5 bg-[#05050a]/90 rounded-2xl overflow-hidden my-3 shadow-md max-w-full text-left font-mono">
      <div className="flex items-center justify-between px-4 py-2 border-b border-white/5 bg-[#090910] text-[10px] text-slate-500 font-bold uppercase tracking-wider">
        <span>{language}</span>
        <button 
          onClick={handleCopy}
          className="flex items-center gap-1 hover:text-white transition-colors cursor-pointer"
        >
          {copied ? (
            <>
              <Check size={11} className="text-emerald-400" />
              <span className="text-emerald-400">Copied!</span>
            </>
          ) : (
            <>
              <Copy size={11} />
              <span>Copy code</span>
            </>
          )}
        </button>
      </div>
      <div className="p-4 overflow-x-auto text-[11px] text-[#39FF14] whitespace-pre select-text leading-relaxed">
        <code>{code}</code>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
};
```

Here is a summary of the implementation:

### 1. ChatGPT-Style Workspace Layout
* **Left Sidebar (Desktop & Mobile Slide-out):**
  * **`+ New Chat` Button:** Starts a fresh empty session instantly.
  * **Saved Conversations:** Lists all past chat threads synced from the Firestore `users_chats` collection. You can load any conversation by clicking it or delete it via the Trash icon.
  * **Drawers & Controls:** Compact links to open the "Teach Semmy" form or the "Browse Registry" explorer as sleek slide-over drawers from the right.
  * **Gemini API Key:** Card at the bottom of the sidebar to manage API keys securely in `localStorage`.
  * **User Profile & Sign Out:** Displays the authenticated Google profile picture and email with a sign-out trigger.
* **Central Chat Window:**
  * **Suggestions Grid (New Chats):** Displays 4 cards (Von Neumann, Logic Gates, Class 11 Accounting, Class 12 Current Electricity) to trigger study questions instantly.
  * **Bouncing Typing Indicator:** Clean pulsing dot animations when Semmy/Gemini is preparing a response.
  * **Floating Input Bar:** Centered at the bottom with standard character limits and a clean submit arrow.

### 2. Custom Markdown & Code Highlights
* Parses headers (`### `, `## `), bold text (`**`), and numbered/bulleted lists into clean React structures.
* Fenced code blocks (` ``` `) are rendered inside an editor container with copy buttons that give visual feedback when clicked.

### 3. Firestore Cloud Persistence
* Chat logs are persistent and tied directly to the user's UID inside the Firestore `users_chats` collection.
* The first message of a new chat is parsed to auto-title the thread in the list view.

Because the app requires **Google Authentication** to access the courses:

### 🤖 Antigravity AI

Let's see: in `useEffect` at lines 276-300, we should support restoring guest mode too!
If there is no Firebase user, but `nextsem_user` in `localStorage` is `offline`, we should set `currentUser` to that offline profile so it persists on page reload in the Semmy AI Chat page!
Yes!
```typescript
  // Listen to Auth State
  useEffect(() => {
    // Check offline user on init
    try {
      const savedUser = localStorage.getItem("nextsem_user");
      if (savedUser) {
        const parsed = JSON.parse(savedUser);
        if (parsed?.uid === "offline") {
          setCurrentUser(parsed);
          setAuthLoading(false);
        }
      }
    } catch (_) {}

    const unsubscribe = onAuthStateChanged(auth, (user) => {
      setAuthLoading(true);
      if (user) {
        const profile = {
          uid: user.uid,
          name: user.displayName || "User",
          email: user.email || "",
          avatar: user.photoURL || ""
        };
        setCurrentUser(profile);
        localStorage.setItem("nextsem_user", JSON.stringify(profile));
        fetchThreads(user.uid);
      } else {
        // Only clear if not in offline mode
        const savedUser = localStorage.getItem("nextsem_user");
        let isOffline = false;
        if (savedUser) {
          try {
            isOffline = JSON.parse(savedUser).uid === "offline";
          } catch(e){}
        }
        if (!isOffline) {
          setCurrentUser(null);
          localStorage.removeItem("nextsem_user");
          setThreads([]);
          setActiveThreadId(null);
          setMessages([]);
        }
      }
      setAuthLoading(false);
    });
    return () => unsubscribe();
  }, []);
```
Oh! This is exceptionally smart! It avoids clearing the offline guest session when the page loads and Firebase Auth detects `user === null` (since a guest has no active Firebase Auth session, Firebase triggers `onAuthStateChanged` with `null`, which would otherwise evict their guest session!).
By checking `isOffline` before clearing, the guest session is preserved perfectly!

### What I Changed:

#### 1. Optional Guest Login (Home & Semmy AI)
* **On the Main Landing Page ([HomeClient.tsx](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/HomeClient.tsx)):** 
  * Re-enabled guest mode by deleting the offline session eviction block.
  * Added a **"Continue as Guest (No Cloud Sync) ➔"** button below the primary Google sign-in button.
* **On the Semmy AI Chat Page ([SemmyAiClient.tsx](file:///c:/jbrs-landing-page/src/app/bca/semmy-ai/SemmyAiClient.tsx)):**
  * Removed the hard block auth gate when `!currentUser` is detected.
  * Added a **"Continue as Guest (No Cloud Sync) ➔"** button beneath the Google login button.
  * Configured state synchronization to preserve local guest sessions across page reloads (by checking if the guest profile `uid: "offline"` exists in `localStorage` before clearing the auth state).

#### 2. Progress Reminders & Banners
* **Homepage (Desktop & Mobile):**
  * When in Guest Mode, a clean glassmorphic banner appears at the very top of both the desktop layout and mobile viewports, displaying:
    > 💡 **Running in Guest Mode**
    > *Connect with your Google account to save your learning history, points, and certificates across web and mobile apps!* `[Sign In with Google]`
* **Semmy AI Chat area:**
  * When in Guest Mode, a notification banner sits at the top of the chat messages panel, reminding users that their conversations will not be persisted in the cloud unless they log in.

Now I have a complete picture. The issue is clear:

- The desktop wrapper at line 2080 uses `overflow-y-auto` → vertical scroll
- The inner `Segmented Modules Layout` at line 2126 uses `overflow-y-auto space-y-10 pb-12` → even more vertical scroll
- Cards have large `h-32` illustration areas and heavy padding, making them too tall

**The fix:** Convert to a fully no-scroll viewport layout:
1. Outer wrapper → `overflow-hidden` (no vertical scroll)
2. Header + compact stats strip → slim, fixed height
3. Both module sections → **horizontal scrolling rows** (`flex gap-4 overflow-x-auto`) with compact cards
4. Footer → slim, inline

This gives a Netflix/dashboard "shelf" look — everything fits without any vertical scroll.

The desktop homepage now uses a **fully no-vertical-scroll viewport layout**:

---

### What Changed

| Before | After |
|---|---|
| `overflow-y-auto` — full vertical scroll | `overflow-hidden` — zero vertical scroll |
| Tall card grid with `h-32` SVG illustrations | Compact `w-52` cards in **horizontal scroll rows** |
| Two stacked grid sections requiring 2+ screens | Two neat **shelf rows** that fit within one viewport |
| Bloated header with `pt-10 pb-4` | Compact slim header `pt-6 pb-3` |
| Footer stacked on two lines with large `py-6` | Single-line footer `py-3` pinned to bottom |
| Cards needed clicks on a separate `<button>` | Entire card is clickable (`onClick` on the card `div`) |

### Layout Structure (Desktop)
```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ [Guest Mode Banner — only if offline]               │  ~2.5rem  
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 🎓 Nextsem Academy             Done: 3 | 🪙 Pts: 80 │  ~4rem
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 📚 Structured Core Curriculums                      │
│ [Card]  [Card]  [Card]  [Card]  [Card] ──────────►  │  ~40%
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ⚡ Interactive Sandboxes & AI Tools                  │
│ [Card]  [Card]  [Card] ──────────────────────────►  │  ~40%
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ © 2026 Nextsem · About · Contact · Privacy · Terms  │  ~2rem
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Both card shelves scroll **horizontally** if there are too many items, but the **page itself never scrolls vertically**. The `.no-scrollbar` CSS utility hides the horizontal scrollbar while keeping swipe/drag functionality intact.

### 🤖 Antigravity AI

I have completed the entire set of tasks and deployed them successfully to both production and staging.

Here is a summary of the new improvements:

### 1. Optional Google Sign-In with Data Reminders
We removed the compulsory Google authentication, making it completely optional while adding smart reminders to prevent data loss:
* **Terminal Landing Page (`HomeClient.tsx`):** If a user clicks **"Continue as Guest"**, a modal alerts them that their navigation checkpoints, completed chapters, and XP will only be saved locally in their browser. It reminds them to use Google Sign-In if they want their progress synced between the website and the mobile app.
* **Semmy AI Page (`SemmyAiClient.tsx`):** Clicking **"Continue as Guest"** triggers a warning dialog advising the student that their AI chat threads will not sync to the cloud or other devices without a Google account.

### 2. Fully Dedicated NCERT PDF Viewer & Downloader Workspace
We overhauled the `/ncert/pdf` page into a fully-fledged dashboard workspace:
* **Interactive Library Sidebar:** Added a Class Filter Tab (`All` / `11th` / `12th`) and an expandable lists panel on the left containing all subjects and chapters. Users can switch books instantly in one place.
* **Google Docs PDF Wrapper:** External NCERT URLs are now wrapped inside Google's Docs viewer, bypassing `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN` block policies to render the live textbook directly inside our iframe.
* **Smart Source Toggles:** The top bar displays a source status badge (`Live · NCERT` or `Local Backup`) and provides a button to switch between live and local files manually with a single click.
* **Direct Downloader:** Renders raw PDF downloads of the textbooks on click.

### 3. Verification & Live Deployment
* Compiles cleanly with the Next.js static build system (`npm run build`).
* Deployed live to Production: **[https://nextsem.online/ncert/pdf](https://nextsem.online/ncert/pdf)**
* Deployed live to Staging: **[https://nextsem-staging.web.app/ncert/pdf](https://nextsem-staging.web.app/ncert/pdf)**
* Saved and archived the conversation history.

```typescript
  const handleGuestLaunch = (mod: ModuleItem) => {
    // If not logged in, trigger handlePlayOffline first (which will show the confirmation dialog)
    // and if they accept, route them to the course!
    if (!currentUser) {
      const confirmGuest = window.confirm(
        "⚠️ Guest Mode Reminder\n\nIf you continue without Google, your progress will only be saved locally on this browser. To keep it synced across the website and mobile app, we recommend signing in with Google.\n\nDo you want to continue as Guest?"
      );
      if (!confirmGuest) return;

      // Perform guest sign in
      setIsOfflineMode(true);
      const offlineProfile = {
        uid: "offline",
        name: "Offline Player",
        email: "offline@nextsem.local",
        avatar: ""
      };
      setCurrentUser(offlineProfile);
      localStorage.setItem("nextsem_user", JSON.stringify(offlineProfile));
    }
    
    // Launch course
    const modPhase = coursePhases.find(p => p.id === mod.id.toUpperCase());
    const path = modPhase?.topics[0]?.path || mod.firstChapterId;
    handleLaunchChapter(path);
  };
```

  };

  const filteredModules = modules.filter(mod => {
    if (!searchQuery) return true;
    const q = searchQuery.toLowerCase();
    return (
      mod.title.toLowerCase().includes(q) ||
      mod.subtitle.toLowerCase().includes(q) ||
      mod.description.toLowerCase().includes(q)
    );
  });

  if (!currentUser) {
    const previousSessions = getPreviousSessions();

    return (
      <div className="w-full min-h-screen bg-[#0e0d0b] text-white flex flex-col font-sans overflow-hidden relative">
        {/* Subtle grid backdrop with warm tint */}
        <div className="absolute inset-0 pointer-events-none z-0"
          style={{ backgroundImage: "linear-gradient(rgba(217,119,6,0.015) 1px,transparent 1px),linear-gradient(90deg,rgba(217,119,6,0.015) 1px,transparent 1px)", backgroundSize: "60px 60px" }} />

        {/* Ambient bronze/emerald radial glow */}
        <div className="absolute top-[-10%] left-[-10%] w-[55vw] h-[55vw] rounded-full blur-[140px] bg-emerald-500/5 pointer-events-none z-0" />
        <div className="absolute bottom-[-10%] right-[-10%] w-[50vw] h-[50vw] rounded-full blur-[140px] bg-amber-500/5 pointer-events-none z-0" />

        {/* Top Navigation Bar */}
        <nav className="relative z-10 flex items-center justify-between px-6 py-4 border-b border-stone-900 bg-black/10 backdrop-blur-md">
          <div className="flex items-center gap-2.5">
            <img src="/logo.svg" alt="Nextsem Logo" className="w-8 h-8 rounded-xl border border-emerald-500/20" />
            <span className="font-serif text-lg font-black text-white tracking-wide">
              Nextsem <span className="text-amber-500 font-normal">Academy</span>
            </span>
          </div>
          <button
            onClick={handleSignIn}
            className="flex items-center gap-2 px-4 py-2 bg-gradient-to-r from-amber-600 to-amber-700 hover:from-amber-500 hover:to-amber-600 border border-amber-600/35 rounded-xl text-[10px] font-black uppercase tracking-wider text-white transition-all cursor-pointer shadow-sm"
          >
            Sign in
          </button>
        </nav>

        {/* Dynamic AdSense Header Slot */}
        <div className="relative z-10 w-full max-w-4xl mx-auto px-6 mt-3">
          <div className="w-full bg-[#151310]/80 border border-stone-900/60 rounded-xl p-2.5 flex items-center justify-center text-[10px] text-stone-600 font-bold tracking-widest uppercase">
            Sponsored Ad Slot
          </div>
        </div>

        {/* Core Layout Split Section */}
        <div className="relative z-10 flex-1 w-full max-w-7xl mx-auto px-6 py-6 flex flex-col lg:flex-row gap-8 items-center justify-between min-h-0 overflow-hidden">
          
          {/* Left Panel: Hero and Search input */}
          <div className="w-full lg:w-[45%] flex flex-col text-left shrink-0 max-w-xl">
            {/* Classy Badge */}
            <div className="inline-flex items-center gap-2 w-max px-3.5 py-1.5 rounded-full bg-amber-500/10 border border-amber-500/20 text-amber-500 text-[10px] font-black uppercase tracking-widest mb-5">
              <span className="w-1.5 h-1.5 rounded-full bg-amber-500 animate-pulse" />
              ⚡ Premium Coding Workstation
            </div>

            <h1 className="text-4xl md:text-5xl font-serif text-white tracking-tight leading-[1.1] mb-4">
              Master CLI Terminal &amp;<br />
              <span className="text-transparent bg-clip-text bg-gradient-to-r from-amber-400 to-amber-600">
                Frontend Development
              </span>
            </h1>
            
            <p className="text-slate-400 text-sm md:text-base leading-relaxed mb-8">
              Step-by-step interactive command-line workspace. Run files, write structures, test your knowledge, and earn your certification.
            </p>

            {/* Interactive W3Schools-style Search Bar */}
            <div className="w-full relative mb-6">
              <input
                type="text"
                value={searchQuery}
                onChange={(e) => setSearchQuery(e.target.value)}
                placeholder="Search subjects, e.g. HTML, dir, React..."
                className="w-full px-5 py-4 rounded-2xl bg-[#14120f] border border-stone-800 focus:border-amber-600 focus:outline-none text-sm text-white placeholder-stone-600 font-sans transition-all shadow-inner"
              />
              {searchQuery && (
                <button
                  onClick={() => setSearchQuery("")}
                  className="absolute right-4 top-1/2 -translate-y-1/2 text-stone-500 hover:text-white text-[9px] font-black uppercase tracking-widest"
                >
                  Clear
                </button>
              )}
            </div>

            {/* Primary Google Login CTA */}
            <div className="flex flex-col sm:flex-row gap-3 items-center">
              <button
                onClick={handleSignIn}
                className="group flex items-center justify-center gap-3 w-full sm:w-auto px-6 py-3.5 bg-white hover:bg-slate-50 text-black font-black text-xs uppercase tracking-widest rounded-xl cursor-pointer transition-all shadow-lg hover:scale-[1.01]"
              >
                <svg className="w-4 h-4 shrink-0" viewBox="0 0 24 24">
                  <path fill="#EA4335" d="M12 5.04c1.62 0 3.08.56 4.22 1.65l3.15-3.15C17.45 1.74 14.93 1 12 1 7.37 1 3.4 3.66 1.48 7.55l3.77 2.92C6.15 7.42 8.87 5.04 12 5.04z" />
                  <path fill="#4285F4" d="M23.49 12.27c0-.81-.07-1.59-.2-2.36H12v4.51h6.46c-.29 1.48-1.14 2.73-2.4 3.58l3.76 2.92c2.2-2.03 3.67-5.01 3.67-8.65z" />
                  <path fill="#FBBC05" d="M5.25 14.75c-.25-.76-.39-1.57-.39-2.41s.14-1.65.39-2.41L1.48 7.02C.54 8.92 0 11.04 0 13.25c0 2.21.54 4.33 1.48 6.23l3.77-2.92z" />
                  <path fill="#34A853" d="M12 23c3.24 0 5.97-1.07 7.96-2.91l-3.76-2.92c-1.1.74-2.52 1.18-4.2 1.18-3.13 0-5.85-2.38-6.75-5.43L1.48 15.8C3.4 19.69 7.37 23 12 23z" />
                </svg>
                Continue with Google
              </button>
            </div>
            
            <p className="text-[10px] text-stone-600 mt-3 max-w-sm leading-normal">
              Sync and persist progress automatically to the cloud, or proceed directly by selecting a chapter.
            </p>

            {/* Previous Sessions (Re-log helper) */}
            {previousSessions.length > 0 && (
              <div className="mt-6 border-t border-stone-900 pt-5">
                <span className="text-[9px] font-black text-amber-500/80 uppercase tracking-widest block mb-2.5">💾 Continue Session</span>
                <div className="space-y-2">
                  {previousSessions.map((session) => (
                    <div key={session.email} className="flex items-center justify-between p-2 rounded-xl bg-[#14120f] border border-stone-900">
                      <span className="text-[10px] font-bold text-slate-300 truncate max-w-[200px]">{session.email}</span>
                      <button onClick={handleSignIn} className="text-[9px] font-black uppercase text-amber-500 hover:text-amber-400">
                        Launch
                      </button>
                    </div>
                  ))}
                </div>
              </div>
            )}
          </div>

          {/* Right Panel: Compact, scrollable modules catalog */}
          <div className="flex-1 w-full h-full overflow-y-auto max-h-[70vh] lg:max-h-[76vh] pr-2 custom-scrollbar space-y-3 z-10 mt-6 lg:mt-0">
            {/* Header native message */}
            <div className="flex items-center justify-between select-none px-1">
              <span className="text-[9px] font-black text-stone-500 uppercase tracking-wider">Available Chapters ({filteredModules.length})</span>
              <span className="text-[8px] font-bold text-emerald-400 bg-emerald-500/10 border border-emerald-500/20 px-2 py-0.5 rounded-full uppercase tracking-widest">Optional Login</span>
            </div>

            {filteredModules.map((mod) => {
              const isSandbox = mod.category === "sandbox";
              return (
                <div
                  key={mod.id}
                  onClick={() => handleGuestLaunch(mod)}
                  className="w-full bg-[#14120f]/95 hover:bg-[#191713]/90 border border-stone-900 hover:border-amber-600/30 rounded-2xl p-4 flex items-center justify-between gap-4 cursor-pointer transition-all hover:shadow-[0_4px_20px_rgba(217,119,6,0.03)]"
                >
                  <div className="flex items-center gap-3.5 min-w-0">
                    <div
                      className="w-10 h-10 rounded-xl flex items-center justify-center border text-lg shrink-0"
                      style={{
                        borderColor: `${mod.color}25`,
                        backgroundColor: `${mod.color}08`
                      }}
                    >
                      {mod.id === "cli" ? "🪟" : mod.id === "bca" ? "🎓" : mod.id === "ncert" ? "📖" : isSandbox ? "⚙️" : "📝"}
                    </div>
                    <div className="text-left min-w-0">
                      <span className="text-[8px] font-black uppercase tracking-widest text-slate-500">
                        {isSandbox ? "Sandbox Workspace" : mod.subtitle.split(" • ")[0]}
                      </span>
                      <h3 className="text-xs font-black text-white truncate mt-0.5 leading-snug">{mod.title}</h3>
                      <p className="text-[10px] text-slate-400 truncate mt-0.5 max-w-sm lg:max-w-md">{mod.description}</p>
                    </div>
                  </div>
                  <button className="px-3.5 py-1.5 bg-[#1f1a14] border border-amber-600/20 hover:border-amber-600/40 text-amber-500 rounded-lg text-[9px] font-black uppercase tracking-wider shrink-0 transition-all">
                    Start
                  </button>
                </div>
              );
            })}

            {/* In-feed native sponsor ad */}
            <div className="w-full bg-[#151310]/40 border border-dashed border-stone-900 rounded-2xl py-3 px-4 flex items-center justify-between text-[9px] text-stone-600 font-bold uppercase tracking-wider select-none">
              <span>Sponsor native banner</span>
              <span className="text-[8px] border border-stone-800 px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded">Ad</span>
            </div>
          </div>

        </div>

        {/* Footer */}
        <footer className="relative z-10 text-center py-4 text-[9px] text-stone-600 border-t border-stone-900 select-none mt-auto flex flex-col items-center justify-center gap-1.5 shrink-0 bg-black/10">
          <div>© 2026 Nextsem Academy · Interactive Windows Terminal &amp; Web Development Hub</div>
          <div className="flex flex-wrap justify-center gap-3 text-stone-500 font-bold uppercase tracking-wider text-[8px]">
            <Link href="/about" className="hover:text-white transition-colors">About Us</Link>
            <span>·</span>
            <Link href="/contact" className="hover:text-white transition-colors">Contact</Link>
            <span>·</span>
            <Link href="/privacy" className="hover:text-white transition-colors">Privacy Policy</Link>
            <span>·</span>
            <Link href="/terms" className="hover:text-white transition-colors">Terms of Service</Link>
          </div>
        </footer>

        {/* Custom scrollbar styling block */}
        <style>{`
          .custom-scrollbar::-webkit-scrollbar {
            width: 4px;
          }
          .custom-scrollbar::-webkit-scrollbar-track {
            background: transparent;
          }
          .custom-scrollbar::-webkit-scrollbar-thumb {
            background: rgba(217, 119, 6, 0.12);
            border-radius: 9px;
          }
          .custom-scrollbar::-webkit-scrollbar-thumb:hover {
            background: rgba(217, 119, 6, 0.3);
          }
        `}</style>
      </div>
    );
  }
```

                          onClick={() => {

                              handleLaunchChapter(path);

                            }

                          className="flex items-center gap-3 p-2 rounded-xl hover:bg-white/5 cursor-pointer text-left"

                                router.push(`/${item.path}`);

                        >
                          <span className="text-base">{item.emoji}</span>
                          <div>
                            <span className="block text-[10px] font-black text-white leading-tight">{item.title}</span>
                            <span className="block text-[8px] text-slate-500">{item.subtitle}</span>
                          </div>
                        </div>
                      ))}
                    </div>
                  </div>
                </>
              )}
            </div>

          <div className="flex items-center gap-4 shrink-0">
            {/* Stats (if logged in) */}

                <span className="text-[10px] font-black text-emerald-450 uppercase">{completedCount} Done</span>
                <div className="h-3 w-px bg-stone-800" />
                <span className="text-[10px] font-black text-amber-500 uppercase">🪙 {points} Pts</span>
              </div>

            <button className="p-1.5 hover:bg-white/5 text-slate-400 hover:text-white rounded-lg transition-colors cursor-pointer" title="Toggle Light/Dark Theme">

              <Moon size={14} className="hidden dark:block" />
            </button>

              >
                Sign In
              </button>
            )}
          </div>
          
        </div>
      </header>
    );

```
Wait, if a keyword is part of HTML tags (like `<span class="text-blue-400 font-bold">`), replacing keywords with regex will match the class names or tags themselves!
Wait: `escaped` has already been run through some highlighting by the time keywords are checked, or the other highlights run before keywords.
Wait, let's look at `highlightJS`:
```typescript
  // Highlight strings
  escaped = escaped.replace(/("[^"]*?")/g, '<span class="text-amber-300">$1</span>');
  escaped = escaped.replace(/('[^']*?')/g, '<span class="text-amber-300">$1</span>');
  escaped = escaped.replace(/(`[^`]*?`)/g, '<span class="text-amber-300">$1</span>');
  
  // Highlight single-line comments
  escaped = escaped.replace(/(\/\/[^\n]*)/g, '<span class="text-slate-500 italic">$1</span>');
  
  // Highlight keywords
...
```
If `escaped` has `class="text-amber-300"`, the word `class` doesn't match a keyword, but what if there's `const` in a string? The string gets wrapped in `<span class="text-amber-300">"const"</span>` first, then `const` gets highlighted inside it!
Wait, that is one issue, but let's check: what is "correct this pager"?
Wait, is there a layout issue where the editor textarea does not match the pre tag's styles exactly, causing the text to be misaligned (or double text, or overlay alignment issues)?
Yes! In many browsers, if the transparent textarea and the background `<pre>` tag don't have *exactly* identical font sizes, font families, line heights, paddings, borders, margins, and whitespace handling, the caret and the characters will drift! It looks extremely ugly and broken when the typed text and the highlighted text are misaligned.
Let's see: how can we fix this alignment?
Wait! In modern web design, instead of doing an overlay highlight with textarea, we can simply style the textarea with standard colors, OR we can make sure they align *perfectly* by setting identical CSS styles:
Let's look at the overlay pre tag:
`className="absolute inset-0 p-4 text-slate-300 pointer-events-none whitespace-pre-wrap break-all overflow-y-auto leading-relaxed h-full w-full select-none"`
And the textarea:
`className="absolute inset-0 p-4 bg-transparent focus:outline-none resize-none overflow-y-auto leading-relaxed h-full w-full text-transparent caret-white whitespace-pre-wrap break-all"`
Wait! Let's check `font-family` and other properties:
`leading-relaxed` is a tailwind class. But what about font-family? The container has `font-mono text-xs`. But in Tailwind, `textarea` and `pre` have different default line-heights, letter-spacings, and browser defaults!
Specifically, a textarea has a default font-family (usually browser default monospace) which might not match the `<pre>` tag's font.
Let's look at how to ensure perfect synchronization:
We can specify exact inline styles or style classes for both `pre` and `textarea` to guarantee they are 100% pixel-perfect aligned:
```css
font-family: monospace, Courier, monospace;
font-size: 13px;
line-height: 20px;
padding: 16px;
border: none;
margin: 0;
```
